We proceed with the examination of item 19 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending certain laws in order to improve the legal environment of entrepreneurs (print No 994). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Development of Mr Mariusz Haladyj to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-16-sejm-30-2.u4	Mr. Marshal! Dear Members, The draft law amending certain laws to improve the legal environment of entrepreneurs, which is the subject of today's reading, is part of a package of facilitations for entrepreneurs, which in such a whole composition it has a contractual name for the company. 100 changes for the company. The aim of the project that we are presenting to you today is to facilitate the pursuit of business activities. Of course, there is no need to convince anyone that the quality of this legal environment is one of the factors determining the development of entrepreneurship and the comfort of our entrepreneurs doing business on a daily basis. A number of very detailed rules from different branches of the law are, let's just say, the creativity or the ability of entrepreneurs to devote their time to what is the essence of doing business, and too much time is spent on administrative issues. One of the objectives of this project is to reduce barriers to operating, particularly administrative barriers, at particular points in economic law. The whole package of 100 changes for companies, as we call it, consists of several projects. Maybe I can present this, just so that we can draw a short picture and clarify the situation, and maybe it will be easier to work on this project and on the next ones that will come to Parliament. This package of 100 changes for companies consists of eight projects: seven draft laws and one draft regulation. Today, after the stage of the permanent committee, and before the Council of Ministers, there are three more projects - they are already more homogeneous than today - i.e.: changes in the so-called. creditor package, reform of the measurement system. Before the Committee of the Council of Ministers are still: the succession of family companies, shortening the period of storage of employee files and creating a new legal formula of a simple public limited company. The next projects will be much more homogeneous. Today's project brings together changes in various laws, often point-by-point, identified on the basis - which is probably also important - of talks with entrepreneurs. These are solutions that have emerged as a result of their identification of problems, established in various forms of cooperation, whether at meetings or within the cooperation platforms that we organise in the Ministry, cooperation with business organisations and, of course, cooperation with other ministries. And it is probably worth emphasising that in these works we did not reject ideas that could often seem small, unspectacular or even prosaic. This is also often the case, especially if there are on a mass scale, that entrepreneurs have a problem with them, which also translates a bit later into such, and I apologize for the everyday definition, irritation, in terms of the comfort of doing business. So all the changes that we are proposing are somehow responding to the barriers they have identified in their business practice. To discuss some of the groups of proposals that we are making here, perhaps I would start with changes in the regulation on control or, in principle, restrictions on the control of entrepreneurs. You know that businesses have always shown checks as a source of nuisance. Of course, these controls cannot be eliminated, but what we are proposing is a test. such as balance of interests between inspections and controlled ones. I'm just going to say, speaking of scale, that the scale of tax checks is about 100,000. per year. Health checks or ZUS is also a government of about 80,000. control per year, i.e. it is certainly an area which is for entrepreneurs, probably especially the smaller ones, felt. Thus, in the Law on the Freedom of Business, we have foreseen, first, a solution which will oblige the authorities to plan and carry out checks, but after having established the likelihood of a breach of the rules, i.e. to do so. risk analysis so that controls better identify these areas before the start of the audit, in fact, in order to reduce the number of controls in those businesses that do not generate such risks. This is a plus and for entrepreneurs themselves, of course, those who conduct their business in a reliable manner, but also seem - from the point of view of the interest of the State or from the point of view of the public interest - also desirable, because then we really focus on those where these risks are greater. Besides, it can be said that this is a trend, which has also been seen for several years, for example in transformations, when it comes to an approach, for example. to tax controls where better identification of entities now results in better collection, better detection without the need to increase the number of controls themselves. The latest figures presented by the Ministry of Finance were very clear. That's what they're showing. Other things in terms of checks are the possibility of introducing, filing a complaint to a voivodship administrative court for the duration of checks, allowing joint checks with the consent of the entrepreneur. It will be especially important in situations where there is, I do not know, the obligation to receive a building by several services, several inspections, then for the company itself it is more convenient for inspections to come together, to pick up this device or object and to deal with this matter at one time, it is from the point of view of the entrepreneur. But we also ban re-control by the same authority of the subject-matter of the audit or, for the sake of clarity of procedures from a controlled point of view, an obligation on the authorities to provide information on control procedures on their websites, in a way that is more user-friendly than a kind of dry reading by traders. Finally, this is also an answer to the demands of entrepreneurs themselves, but also an inspection. We propose to introduce an exception to the notification of the intention to carry out a check, an exception that this condition should not apply to measurements or inspections or sampling, because of course 7 days at such a time gives the possibility to move e.g. goods which are questionable from the point of view of e.g. sanitary rules, and avoid the findings of real checks, and this does not, of course, affect fair trading, fair competition. As far as changes in the Tax Order are concerned, we have a solution which is intended to increase the protection of taxpayers from the negative consequences of changing the interpretation of the rules, so-called. The legal certainty clause, which we have already discussed in the Sejm in the committee on small and medium-sized enterprises. At that time, there was also a call from the SME parliamentary team to introduce this solution. In this amendment, there is a proposal: i.e. entrepreneurs or taxpayers, because this will not only apply to entrepreneurs who did not present, for example. the individual interpretation, but they have complied with the established or dominant interpretation practice of the Minister of Finance, will have protection arising precisely from compliance with this line of interpretation against sanctions from the Treasury. If, I don't know, the approach would change, then taxpayers are protected backwards, possibly the effects of changes in the interpretation of the law by tax authorities would only concern the situation for the future. Another point is that the solution will be particularly important in a situation where, for example, It's only a matter of time before you make a decision about an investment. Then it is actually a week, there are 2 weeks to decide when there is no time to apply for an individual interpretation, then such a taxpayer can examine how the Treasury provides the provision on the basis of the interpretations that are included in the BIP. The second institution is a new institution in Polish legislation, i.e. tax explanations. This will also involve the protection of the taxpayer, which will comply with the explanations issued by the Minister of Finance. Moreover, today such explanations concerning VAT are issued by the Minister of Finance, and they are not covered by the guarantee from their compliance... are numerous, that is to say, disputes before the court as to whether or not the taxpayer protects or does not comply with these explanations. The tax authorities are most often of the opinion that they do not protect, and that is why we are introducing this institution in order to provide protection. And the third point of the law clause is the protection resulting, in quotes, from the checks carried out on VAT rates. So the positive arrangements for my application of the appropriate rate will protect me from the effects of a possible future check, which would have led me to question my method of accounting the rates, the calculation of the appropriate rates for the product in question, so that... And this protection will, of course, cover not only the period that was the subject of the treasury check, but also the period that has already occurred, of course, after the check period. Tax Ordination - another rational change, I think, but also a de facto civilization. We are introducing the principle of adding up time limits at the various stages of the procedure as regards the calculation of interest for delay in case the decision is repealed and the case is referred to again. Thus, the period which will be needed to carry out the additional proceedings, which is de facto due to the error of the body of first instance, will no longer be included in the period during which these interest rates are charged. The other two changes, in terms of financial, tax law, are those related to accounting. Firstly, it is about raising the annual revenue limit to which PIT taxpayers can lead. simplified accounting, from 1.2 million to 2 million euro. The second amendment is to increase the revenue limit for the use of flat-rate business taxation from 150 000. Euros up to 250 000 Euros. These limits have not been valued since 2008. We are just increasing the volume of entrepreneurs who can benefit from these solutions. In the case of simplified accounting, the advantage for one company can be even the government of several thousand zlotys per year plus of course less administrative burden, especially in the case of a lump sum. Work law. In turn, there is an increase in the limit from 20 to 50 employees in terms of the thresholds for the obligation to create an occupational social benefit fund and the rules of remuneration, the rules of employment, and in the workplaces where the occupational union operates, at the request of the employer, the employer will have to issue such rules or create a social benefit fund. This, on the other hand, is also related to this - this is due to our conversations with employers - that in order not to enter into these successive thresholds, entrepreneurs limit in fact employment in the company or divide companies into smaller ones, or simply employ them as a civil contract, so that they are not qualified for these thresholds either. And if there's 50,000 in this interval between 20 and 49. companies, so far in this range 1 to 19 there are 650 thousand. companies. And we are hoping that this will remove one of the barriers to the growth of these companies, so that such obligations do not hold them back from increasing the scale of activity or the scale of employment. And the entry into this threshold of 50 employees - this is already a scale effect - makes these obligations less felt for such companies. And the same is true of the limits, for example, of simplified accounting, so as not to limit revenue. And while again in this range from 1.2 million to 2 million euro there are only a few thousand entrepreneurs, but below this threshold to 1.2 million, we know that this is an excellent majority, ninety-seven percent of entrepreneurs who account for PIT. At the same time, as regards labour matters, we propose an extension and, on the other hand, an equalisation of time limits for the lodging of appeals by the employee, mainly from the termination of the employment contract - from 7 to 21 days, in the event of a request for a return to work in connection with the termination of the contract without notice and a request for a contract of employment - from 14 to 21 days. These different deadlines caused such confusion, above all, among workers. There have been situations of failure to comply with the term precisely because these terms have caused such chaos in terms of understanding or decryption. I think that, from the point of view of employers themselves, better prepared appeals are later beneficial for the efficiency of the procedure, because a longer period is also an opportunity to better express de facto the demands of the employee. Importantly, this is a common request from both employers and workers, and it is acceptance of this solution by the Social Dialogue Council. The amendments also apply to the work certificate so that it is issued only at the request of the worker if the break in employment with the employer is less than 7 days. Today, too, these regulations are not very rational, because after 24 months such a certificate must be issued, even if the employee does not expect it. In turn, for example, if he is walking. on the issue of accident insurance contributions, here also we propose a change which, I would say, rationalises these provisions a little bit, because today we have a very strict provision that if the payer does not transfer the data to the Social Security Office or transmit false data resulting in a reduction in the interest rate of the accident contribution, the Social Security Office determines by decision the interest rate of this premium for the entire year of 150% . We propose that the ZUS first be obliged to inform the payer of shortcomings, errors in his performance of these obligations, and that the payer have the chance to make up for this error, correct or transmit information and that a more rigorous sanction should occur only if he does not do so, i.e. in case of deliberate omission. Many entrepreneurs complained that this particular error, which they are not always fully aware of, has such strict consequences. This will also reduce the burden on the CSO itself, because it will not be necessary to conduct these proceedings. Information obligations on environmental legislation - here are two changes. Today, even small operators who benefit from the environment on a very small scale or place small quantities of packaging waste on the market are under these obligations. Often these entrepreneurs are not even aware of these obligations, and they are exposed to high sanctions. We therefore propose to exempt entrepreneurs who have placed on the market products in packages with a total weight of up to 1 t in a given calendar year from the obligation to carry out a public educational campaign. To illustrate, today he is obliged to carry out such an obligation, that is to conduct a public educational campaign, e.g. a small handicraft that sells mail order of its products or products. Similarly, we propose exemption from reporting obligations to marshals of those who are exempt from the payment of an environmental charge when their annual fee does not exceed PLN 100. That's a bunch of about 100,000. entrepreneurs. For the sake of illustration, I will only say that today entrepreneurs are obliged to do such an obligation, who use, for example, the business. cars - small entrepreneurs. Simplification and shortening of the investment process - here we propose for changes to the Construction Law. exemption of certain works from construction permit or notification. There will be no need to report parts of the buildings, especially economic ones. Similar changes include reconstructions or renovations of construction works - shortening the deadline from 30 to 21 days to object to construction applications. Today we have a deadline of 30 days, and if the authority does not speak within 21 days after any changes, the investor will be able to consider that there is no objection expressed by the authority, i.e. it will be called. Silent handling of the case. Here we would have had these 9 days less, but also a very rational change, in my opinion, to allow the old age to issue certificates of non-objection earlier. The old age itself tells us that sometimes there are situations that they could have issued such a certificate after 2, 5 days, and some, without such a legal basis, fear it. We have also clarified the institution's significant withdrawal from the approved construction project. We propose that no change in the height, width or length of the building, which is of course not a linear object, should be such a significant deviation from the project of any kind, if this deviation does not exceed 2% , of course, under other conditions, for example the absence of an increase in the scope of the object's impact area. That's also an entrepreneur's request. When we first heard about him, we even thought he was quite abstract. The more we talked, the more we heard about this postulate, because what they were complaining about is that in receiving these buildings, the service can examine almost the nanomillimeter with accuracy. compliance with the approved design. We also offered the possibility of using a temporary construction site until the decision on the construction permit was obtained, plus an extension of the term of use from 120 to 180 days. Today, after the end of the term of use, the temporary object must be removed, i.e. it cannot be used any longer. Recent changes in the Construction Law are also a permit to rationalise the activities carried out by the supervision itself in the event of a construction disaster. The rules will, of course, allow, if the High House accepts, one procedure for all buildings damaged in one event, which caused this construction disaster, of course, in order to explain the causes of the disaster more quickly, if, for example. As a result of one fire, an economic building burned down and located next to the garage - just to conduct one procedure rather than a few for each building. The last changes that I would signal are simplifications in technical law. I'd say two, that's just an example. This, first of all, abolishing the obligation of technical testing which precedes the adoption of decisions authorising the operation of the device in the event of a change in the operation of this device - that is how I buy a used device with important tests, so that I do not have to certify it again. A seemingly minor problem, but occurring on a very massive scale. And the second thing is to introduce the possibility for UDT and TDT to repay, defer, pay the debts into instalments. It's also a rational way to manage resources. The point is, firstly, not to conduct proceedings whose costs exceed the financial advantage which can be obtained and, secondly, to enable entrepreneurs who are in difficulty at a given point in time, at least to spread the fee into instalments or... Well, that's probably gonna be the most common situation. Finally, I would like to say absolutely that this initiative, as I said at the beginning, is an element of the process, which consists in such a difficult identification of concrete legal barriers to economic activity. It's a process, you can say, continuous, because these problems are identified by us on an ongoing basis. We, after some time of such an assessment of such a process, gather them into a group of issues and try to find solutions that will solve these problems. As I said at the beginning, some are more systemic, the type of changes in the Tax Order, but - as you Members hear - some are just such points, even prosaic. But what I can say for myself is that they're all such a daily nuisance. What we often talk about in various types of public discussions, panels, conferences, etc., we focus on such things as the most spectacular, the type of taxes, frequency, etc., and these smallest entrepreneurs are most often hurting and the most distressing of everyday life, which later in their mass, the sum of which is focused or reduced by the fact that entrepreneurs feel the whole of this economic law as bureaucratic, cumbersome and so on. Like I said, it's a toiling process. Surely this bill does not solve all the problems of small entrepreneurs either, but it is a step in the process, so that these nuisances - which, according to entrepreneurs, are severe, incriminating or even, as I said at the beginning, annoying for them, and from the point of view of the state, do not fulfil any important function - these barriers are removed. This project is a stage on the path of removing such barriers. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-16-sejm-30-2.u5	Thank you, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. I ask the floor of Wojciech Murdzk, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice. You're welcome, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-16-sejm-30-2.u6	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The draft law amending certain laws to improve the legal environment of entrepreneurs is the first 33 solutions from over 100, which are intended to re-bureaucratize the economy and facilitate the life of entrepreneurs. The aim of the proposed law is to improve the legal conditions for carrying out business activity in Poland. In response to the specific problems encountered in the practice of carrying out business activities and signalled by individual entrepreneurs, as well as business organisations, the project introduces deregulation changes in 18 laws aimed at simplifying the performance of business activities. The proposals are universal in nature. They will have a positive impact on small and medium-sized enterprises in particular. Among the proposed changes, particular attention should be paid to the relaxation of labour law requirements for small entrepreneurs with no more than 50 employees. The increase in employment of more than 19 employees in the current legal state entails a difficult obligation on the part of the employer: creation of an occupational welfare fund, remuneration rules, working rules. As a result, entrepreneurs are afraid to increase employment by blocking their own development. It is therefore proposed to reduce the legal requirements for small businesses by increasing the thresholds for certain obligations from 20 to 50 employees. We have amendments to the Construction Law on the exemption of certain construction works from construction permit or notification in the following cases: construction of ground floor economic buildings with a construction area of up to 35 m2 will not require either construction permit or notification; construction of a world with a construction area of up to 35 m2 provided that two buildings per 500 m2 of plot area are not exceeded; after the amendment, only the notification will be required; construction of exits from national roads and parking bays on these roads now requires construction permit, after the amendment will require only notification; in the case of exits from district roads and communes, the investment will be possible even without notification. The project provides for simplifications concerning the reconstruction of buildings, as well as the renovation of buildings. Amendment of the Accounting Act. Accounting obligations are among the most incriminating entrepreneurs. The income limit to which individuals' income tax payers may keep tax income and income accounts has not been valued since 2008. It is proposed to increase the revenue limit from EUR 1.2 million to EUR 2 million. Raising the threshold will make it easier for at least a few thousand entrepreneurs to operate and will allow significant savings in the area of accounting services. Amendment of the Act on flat-rate income tax on certain income generated by individuals. The aim is to reduce the administrative burden of compliance with tax obligations and those borne by the smallest entities. Increase the current maximum annual income threshold to benefit from flat-rate taxation from revenues recorded from 150 000. Up to 250,000. The euro will simplify tax procedures for a larger group of small businesses. Amendments to the Freedom of Business Act. In order to protect traders from the consequences of changing the interpretation of the rules, it is proposed to provide the same protection as individual interpretations, including those which do not have individual interpretations. In the proposed amendment to the Act, changes in the control of the performance of activities are designed to ensure a better balance between the interests of the entrepreneur and the public interest, thereby strengthening the state's confidence in the citizen and vice versa. It is proposed to introduce rules according to which checks will be carried out on the basis of the identification of the likelihood of a breach of law, so-called. risk analysis, which introduces the possibility to lodge a complaint with the court for the duration of the checks, provisions enabling joint checks to be carried out, introducing a ban on re-checking, requiring information on control procedures for control authorities to be included on their websites. We can see that this is the whole control package. These examples, good examples, are much more in the bill presented. They show very concrete solutions expected by entrepreneurs and announce further good changes. Law and Justice Club [[♪ The bell ♪]] welcomes the draft law presented. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-16-sejm-30-2.u84	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Dear Members and honourable Members, With regard to the question raised regarding the extension of the date of entry into force of the Act of 1 January 2017 to 1 March 2017, I would like to say that we have actually taken such a decision here in a responsible manner in order to give an opportunity for an even better and more thorough review and consultation of the regulations which must be issued for those laws which are now to be adopted. I would like to say that under the National Tax Administration Act 80 regulations are envisaged, including 26 regulations which enter into force on 1 March 2017 and 10 regulations, including nine regulations which enter into force on 1 March 2017. I would like to say that all these projects are practically ready, we are working on them, they are in the process of internal ministerial arrangements, and after the adoption of the law that we are proceeding today, they will be sent for inter-ministerial consultations. At the same time, in order to maintain the continuity of the work of the authorities and to avoid the accumulation of legislative work, 58 regulations issued under the repealed Tax Control Act and the Customs Service Act shall remain in force until the adoption of the Regulation under the KAS Act, but no longer than 12 months or 3 years respectively. Those regulations can also be revised during this period. It shall constitute an Article. 258 Laws introducing the National Tax Administration Act. We discussed this very carefully at the subcommittee meetings, which lasted for 3 days after 9 hours, and for 9 hours in committee as well. The Free and Solidarnia Circle, following the adoption by the Senate of 12 amendments, reiterates its support for the amendment of this law, which aims to bring national legal standards in the field of rail transport into line with the amendment of European Union law on the creation of a single European railway area. The Senate amendments are mostly technical in nature and aim to harmonise the rules in order to ensure the law's terminology consistency. In addition, the senators have introduced amendments aimed at eliminating possible interpretation doubts and clearly prejudicing that the fees for all the activities of the President of the Railway Transport Office set out in the Act are calculated as equivalent to the euro in zlotys. Furthermore, it was considered that the provisions underlying the imposition of fines should be specific in detail so that they do not leave room for interpretation. When amending the Railway Transport Act, it is worth noting the phenomenon of expanding the area of automation and IT management of the railway network. In our view, we need to start working on new regulations on cybersecurity standards necessary to apply in Industry 4.0 solutions. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-16-sejm-30-2.u316	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The bill is not complicated, it is very short. I'll try to brief him. First of all, the purpose of the project. The need for legislative work is due to the need to comply with the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 22 September 2015, sygn. Act SK 21/14, in which the Court held that Article. 418 c.p.c. in so far as five years after the judgment has become final, it does not allow for the reopening of proceedings on grounds of nullity resulting from the infringement of the Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 The Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms is incompatible with Article. Article 77 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 in conjunction with Article. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. The Court has deferred the existing standard by 18 months, which means that the contested provision will expire on 3 April 2017. In that judgment, the Court held that the expiry of the 5-year period provided for in Article. 408 c.p.c. It definitively closes the judicial path to reopening proceedings and blocks the possibility of repealing a judicial decision in the event of a citizen's complaint. It is also important that proceedings before the European Court are generally long-term, so it may happen that the 5-year period referred to in Article. 408, before this international legal protection authority gives a final decision authorising it to lodge a complaint concerning the reopening of proceedings. Solutions proposed by the Ministry of Justice on behalf of the Government: Article. 1 of the proposal to amend the article. 408 c.p.c. extending the 5-year period provided for in this provision by setting a 10-year deadline in its place in all cases where the 5-year period is currently in force, without limiting this amendment only to a case which has been the basis of the judgment of the European Court of Human Rights. This solution was based on the assumption that the introduction of a separate pre-emptive period to resume proceedings only in a case where the grounds for renewal are based on an infringement of the ECHR Convention, would be an asystemic solution for the regulation of the reopening of proceedings. This implies that the grounds for reopening the procedure should only be those reasons for the violation of the Convention as stated in the ECHR judgment, which have already been identified in c.p.c. as grounds for renewal. Consequently, it is not the ECHR's judgment itself that should be the basis for the renewal, but only as a result of the conclusion that the ECHR has infringed the Convention can a specific basis be established for the renewal resulting from k.p.c. The ECHR case law covers various cases of infringement by a court, in particular infringements of other rights and freedoms arising from the Convention, such as freedom of expression, guaranteeing the protection of property. In some cases, judgments declaring an infringement of the Convention are not at all enforceable by the institution's use of reopening proceedings. An example of the most common situation here is an effective complaint about not knowing the case within a reasonable period of time. In other cases, an infringement identified by the ECHR may result from incorrect national legislation contrary to the Convention. A legislative initiative to amend national law and to bring it into line with the Convention would then be appropriate. In turn, if the origin of the violation of the Convention was not the direct action of national legislation in conflict with the Convention, but a judicial decision, then in certain cases the appropriate instrument may and should be different from the action for renewal, measures such as an action against enforcement or an action for the annulment of a final judgment or an action for damages against the State Treasury for the so-called so-called. It's a court-ordered delusion. These legal measures should be used. Then it is not necessary to refer to the institution to resume the procedure. In any event, it is not necessary, from the point of view of the Convention's requirements, to introduce a basis for renewal because of the ECHR judgment itself. This implies that when assessing the scope of the law protected by Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 of the Convention, account must be taken of the possibility of conflicting the legal situation of entities which are parties to a civil case in which there has been a breach of the right to a fair trial by a court. Following the removal of the effects of an infringement protected by Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 of the Convention could result in a further infringement of the Article by reopening the procedure. Article 6 (6) (a) 1, except that in a different form and against the other side of the civil case. This in turn would violate the principle of protection of acquired rights and the principle of legal certainty linked strictly to the consequences of the finality of the judgment. For these reasons, the project, which I have the honour to present, is implementing the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, which I have already mentioned, precisely by extending the time limit for the reopening of proceedings completed by a final decision of a civil court, universal, and not by introducing a separate basis for the renewal of the ECHR judgment. The project is in fact limited to changing the length of the deadline, to introducing a time limit by adopting a 10-year deadline for all cases where the 5-year deadline is currently in force. It is necessary to limit the timely possibility of reopening proceedings as the final judicial decision should become completely stable. The proposed time limit, i.e. 10 years, takes into account the value of the validity of judgments, ensures the stability of civil law relations established on the basis of final judgments of civil courts, but also guarantees the right to resume civil proceedings within a reasonable and realistic period also to the addressees of judgments of the Court of Human Rights. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 2 of the draft is expected to apply to cases in which by the date of entry into force of the amendment the five-year period has not expired since the decision has been valid. Thus, the 10-year term would apply pro futuro to the legal situation at the time of the entry into force of the Act, which corresponds to the classic regulation of tempus registr actum. Thank you very much.

On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform wanted to present its position on the proposal for a law amending the Civil Procedure Code Act, print No 932. Many years ago, many centuries ago, legislators saw that the passing of time was not meaningless to the law. The institution of pastity has been present in the law for many centuries, but, as can be seen during the analysis of various legal solutions, the time which is given to parties - civil law entities, and not only civil law, to exercise their rights is reduced. This is a consequence of changing the pace of life, bringing new ways of communicating, especially at a distance, into human activity, and this must be noticed and seen at this point. Article 4 is replaced by the following: 408 The Civil Procedure Code in its present form closes the way to the courts of those who seek to reopen the proceedings 5 years after the judgment has been enforced. This five-year period, the pre-emptive term does not deprive the parties of the right to take certain actions in two cases: firstly, if they were deprived of their ability to act, and secondly, in the second case where they were not properly represented. But as the Constitutional Court's judgment shows, that's not enough. Against the background of the provision of the article. 408 The Code of Civil Procedure can be seen as a conflict of two interests: firstly, the continuity of judicial decisions, which favours this 5-year period laid down in the provision of the Article. 408 Code of Civil Procedure, and the right of the party to a fair trial, which is guaranteed inter alia. in Article 5, paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 One of our constitutions. There is therefore a dilemma, which is more important: leaving a defective judgment in legal circulation or giving the citizen the right to a fair judgment. It seems that a fair solution should be sought in this area, and this is where the proposed amendment of the Article is headed. 408 Code of Civil Procedure. But in the current situation, according to the wording of the article. 408 Code of Civil Procedure, 5 years after the judgment was passed, the current wording does not allow for the reopening of proceedings on grounds of nullity resulting, inter alia. the infringement of Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 of the Convention on the Protection of Human Rights and the Citizens. The Constitutional Tribunal considered that this provision was incompatible with the Article. Article 77 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 in conjunction with Article. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland, and thus - as mentioned earlier - will lose its power after 18 months. The proposed amendment therefore extends the 5-year period provided for in Article. 408 and replacing it with a 10-year term. This procedure has among others. allow the parties to proceedings before the European Court of Human Rights to request the reopening of the main proceedings at the end of the proceedings before the European Court, which is generally - and also mentioned - of a rather lengthy procedure, as a result of which, pending the European Court's decision, the parties would lose the possibility of reopening the proceedings in the main proceedings in view of the expiry of time. During the consultation work, comments on the proposed amendment were made, inter alia. The National Judicial Council and the Supreme Court, which suggest the conduct of the provision of the Article. 408 Code of Civil Procedure for the 5-year term and proposed in the revision of the article. 408 extending the deadline to 10 years is proposed only to refer to the judgments of the European Court of Human Rights. That is to say, this gateway, which is provided for by the current wording in two cases allowing the parties to resume the proceedings, is extended by this third case relating to the judgments of the European Court of Human Rights. Leaving a 5-year deadline for cases that will end before national authorities would ensure the stability of the decisions issued, and that is, I think, an important value in itself. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-16-sejm-30-2.u321	Thank you very much. Mrs Barbara Dolniak, Modern.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-16-sejm-30-2.u322	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Art. 408 The Code of Civil Procedure is incompatible with Article. Article 77 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 in conjunction with Article. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1st Constitution. This is what the Constitutional Tribunal ruled in its judgment of 22 September 2015. SK 21/14. The Constitutional Tribunal has held that non-compliance is to the extent that, 5 years after the judgment has become final, it cannot be requested to reopen proceedings on grounds of nullity resulting from the infringement of the Article. 6 of the Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms, as finally decided by the European Court of Human Rights. In its statement of reasons, the Constitutional Tribunal indicated that it was a pre-emptive term in the Article. 408 The Code of Civil Procedure shall be restricted by those applicants who have brought an action for the reopening of proceedings in order to comply with the judgment of the European Court of Human Rights and have based themselves on the nullity referred to in Article. 401 point 1 c.p.c. At the same time, the Constitutional Tribunal pointed out that they were not limited by those complainants who, in order to comply with the judgment of the European Court of Human Rights, based their actions for reopening proceedings under the circumstances set out in the Article. 401 point 2 c.p.c. In the view of the Constitutional Tribunal, such differentiation of the status of complainants is incompatible with the principle of proportionality of the restriction of civil liberties and rights resulting from the Constitution. In addition, the Constitutional Tribunal stated that the five-year time limit for bringing an action for reopening proceedings was too short and therefore the existing provision constitutes an excessive restriction of the right to court. Art. 408 c.p.c. in the version proposed in the draft does not remove this non-constitutional proportionality of the different legal situation of the applicants. The ten-year deadline will continue to limit those applicants who, in their action to reopen proceedings for the execution of the judgment of the European Court of Human Rights, have based it on the nullity referred to in Article. Point 401 of the CR Code, and at the same time it will not be restricted by the complainants who have relied on the complaint under Article. 401 point 2 c.p.c. Therefore, it can be expected that the proposed provision will be considered to be contrary to the Constitution. The amendments proposed in the draft go much further than the Constitutional Court's judgment indicates, as they cover the issues that the Constitutional Tribunal has decided on, as well as introduce a general rule on the change of 5-year term to 10-year term. In addition, the draft provides for the possibility of applying a 10-year period to cases completed before the entry into force of the amendment to the law. This is a solution that can reconcile the principle of certainty of judicial decisions. Instead, it seems that it would be better to accept the arguments set out in the statement of reasons of the Constitutional Tribunal's judgment and to introduce - separate from those currently laid down in the Article. 408 c.p.c. limitation by a binding date - entitlement to bring an action for reopening proceedings on grounds of nullity, the reason for which would be an infringement of the Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 of the Conventions established by the judgment of the European Court of Human Rights. The justification of the project shows that it is an asystem solution and cannot be accepted. C.p.c. there are solutions which refer directly to the judgments of a particular court: Article. 401, Art. 4 c.p.c. In its judgment, the Constitutional Tribunal suggested extending the statutory conditions for reopening proceedings to all cases of breaches of the Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms, but only a separate regulation of the time limit thus limiting the possibility of bringing a complaint motivated by such an infringement. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 of the Conventions at the disposal of Article. 401 c.p.c. It also follows from the justification of the project that such a change would introduce new grounds for reopening the procedure. That's wrong reasoning. Based on the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal and taking into account the judgments of the European Court of Human Rights, conclusions can be drawn: a 10-year period is sufficient for the handling of a complaint by the European Court of Human Rights and if the suggestion of the Constitutional Tribunal is to be applied from the grounds cited above. the judgment, it seems that it would be better to introduce a shorter period of time, which runs from the time when the European Court of Human Rights issued a judgment declaring an infringement. I hope that, during the work of the committee, we will be able, Minister, to discuss and work out a solution that it will actually regulate in accordance with the law. [[♪ The bell ♪]] this issue and at the same time will not differentiate the parties on a case-by-case basis and threaten to be unconstitutional. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-16-sejm-30-2.u330	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present the position of the Kukiz Club15 regarding the government draft law amending the Civil Procedure Code Act, print No 932. In trying to assess the draft submitted, first of all, it is important to commend, first, for the execution of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, which does not cause any major controversy, second, for the time limit for the process of the project in question. It should be noted that Article. 408 c.p.c. it is not effective until April 2017, while we are already dealing with this issue in November 2016. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-16-sejm-30-2.u331	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club to present our position on the government draft law amending the Code of Civil Procedure Act. Today's amendment concerns an acceptable time limit for reopening civil proceedings. This is an exceptional remedy which allows for the removal from legal trading of defective final judgments terminating proceedings in a substantive manner. This measure undermines the stability of final judgments in civil matters and is therefore limited to the most serious deficiencies that have occurred in the course of the proceedings. The reason for the request to reopen the procedure may be, inter alia, based on a false document, the presence of an unauthorised person in the court or the lack of adequate representation of the party during the proceedings. The draft under discussion today results from the need to comply with the judgment of the Constitutional Court of 22 September 2015. In that judgment, the Constitutional Tribunal held that the five-year pre-emptive period, the expiry of which ultimately closes the judicial route to reopen the proceedings, infringes the Article. Article 77 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2nd Constitution of the Republic of Poland. So far, there have been situations where, after lengthy proceedings, the European Court of Human Rights has opened its way to bringing an action for reopening proceedings, but due to a time limit of 5 years such a request could not be taken into account. This problem was noted by the Constitutional Tribunal, recognising the Article. 408 The Civil Procedure Code, which 5 years after the judgment was passed, does not allow for the renewal of proceedings, except for a few exceptions, to be incompatible with the Constitution. At the same time, the Court stressed that there cannot be a situation where, despite the rightness of the citizen's complaint, he has no opportunity to re-examine the case. The new solution adopted is intended to ensure effective legal protection for all citizens affected by judicial authorities. Project in Art. 408 The Civil Procedure Code adds the following: "Art. 408. After the expiry of ten years, the judgment may not be renewed, except where the party has been deprived of the power of action or has not been duly represented. The proposed amendment will facilitate the exercise of its rights by citizens who have turned their affairs over to the European Court of Human Rights. The ECHR's proceedings can last for many years, hence the five-year period for the possibility of reopening judicial proceedings seems to be far from sufficient. This threatens a situation in which the citizen, holding the ECHR ruling in his hand, will in practice not be able to implement it. The proposed period of 10 years will fully safeguard the interests of those who have brought matters to the judgment of the ECHR in Strasbourg and all others who are looking for the possibility of reopening their cases. The solution in which this 10-year period applies not only to cases dealt with by the Court in Strasbourg, but also to all cases where the 5-year period currently applies. First, the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal is implemented in this way. Secondly, this solution will prevent situations where the time limit provided for in Article. 408 c.p.c. may be less than the time limit for the submission of claims for damages - 3 years from the date on which the injured party finds out about the damage and the person liable to remedy it, but not more than 10 years from the date on which the injurious event occurred. The proposed solution takes into account both the need to balance the principle of protection of acquired rights and procedural justice and the principle of binding international law and the right to court. It shall also take into account the value of the validity of judgments. On the one hand, it will guarantee citizens the right to resume civil proceedings within a reasonable and realistic period of time, and on the other hand it will safeguard the stability of civil relations established on the basis of final judgments of civil courts. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the PSL Parliamentary Club, I request that the project be submitted for further work.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-16-sejm-30-2.u332	Marshal of the Sejm pursuant to Art. 87. 2 in conjunction with Article. 90. 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm are addressed by the Government's draft law amending the Civil Procedure Code, contained in print No 932, to the Extraordinary Commission on amendments to the codifications for consideration. We proceed with the examination of item 26. of the agenda: First reading of the parliamentary draft law on amending the Law on Research Institutes (print No. 995). I ask Mrs Lydia Burzyńska to present the reasons for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-16-sejm-30-2.u333	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Draft Print Act No 995 concerns the amendment of certain provisions of the Law of 30 April 2010 on Research Institutes in order to strengthen the supervision of the Minister of Supervision, in particular as regards: firstly, the appointment and cancellation of the Director of the Institute and its Deputy, secondly, matters relating to the day-to-day management of the Research Institute in the event of a failure or cancellation of the Director, thirdly, the possibility of repealing resolutions of the Scientific Council which are not in conformity with the laws and statutes. The current regulations prevent effective supervision of research institutes where the Director does not perform his duties properly. This is particularly dangerous in the case of institutes performing tasks which are particularly important for the planning and implementation of state policy. The draft law assumes that the supervising minister shall appoint and dismiss the Director of the Research Institute without the participation of the Scientific Council. This option gives the Minister greater opportunities to choose the right person for the most important position in the institution, thereby guaranteeing the highest quality of management. The draft also provides that the Supervisory Minister may appoint not only the current Deputy Director but also another person fulfilling the conditions laid down in the Act to act as Director. In the event of the cancellation of the Director of the Institute, it is often the case that the reasons for his appeal by the Minister of Supervision are also valid with regard to his deputy. At the same time, the appointment of a Deputy Director to act as Director could affect the principles of the rule of law, integrity and economy which must be respected in the activities of the research institute. It should be borne in mind that at present the competent minister cannot revoke the Deputy Director of the Institute. Only the Director, the person acting as Director or the person acting as Head of the Institute may do so. It is therefore appropriate to confer on the Supervisory Minister the power to appoint and remove the Deputy Director of the Institute. In the application of the provisions of the present law, please state the law may happen if the supervising minister cancels the Director of the Institute or does not appoint a candidate designated by the competition committee, the former Deputy Director will refuse to accept or perform the duties of Director. In this case, we are dealing de facto with the paralysis of the research institute's activities, which then does not have an authority empowered to act as an executive body. The solution envisaged in the project is intended to prevent this undesirable paralysis of the research institute. It should be stressed that such a person will have to meet practically the same requirements as the Director of the Research Institute, with the exception of the foreign language requirement. This is due to technical difficulties in verifying the knowledge of a foreign language in the event of a sudden need to designate such a person. However, the Deputy Director, who under the current rules must be made mandatory by the Minister of Supervision to perform the duties of Director or Head of Institute, does not have to meet all the conditions laid down in Article. Paragraph 24 6th Bill. In this way, the project aims to improve the quality of the management of the research institute during the transitional period of the absence of its director. The draft also provides that in the event of a reorganisation of the Institute consisting of a change in the subject matter or scope of its operation, the Supervisory Minister may revoke the former Director and appoint a person to carry out his duties. In accordance with the applicable Article. Article 30 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 The Scientific Council, of the number specified in the Institute's Statute, shall consist of: staff of the Institute's scientific and research and technical staff, representing at least 50% of the composition of the Board and persons from outside the Institute, representing between 30% and 50% of the composition of the Board, which in accordance with paragraph. 4 shall be appointed by the supervising minister. In order to strengthen, as I said at the outset, the Minister's supervision is proposed to change these proportions in such a way that the Institute's staff represent at least 40% of the composition of the Council and the persons appointed by the Minister of Supervision represent at least 50%. At the same time, it is proposed that the Chairman of the Scientific Council should be elected only from among the members of the Scientific Council appointed by the Minister for Supervision. New regulations have also been added concerning the examination of legality and the statute of the Institute of resolutions of the Scientific Council. The proposed regulation is intended to ensure that the supervisory minister is able to exercise effective supervision of the Institute's activities. In order to facilitate the establishment, division, merger and reorganisation of institutes, it is proposed to shorten the period for the presentation by the working group of opinions from 3 to 2 months from the date of its appointment to the Minister. The draft law has a positive socio-economic impact and does not have financial implications and does not provide for public expenditure. This project is not covered by European Union law. On behalf of the applicants, I ask the High House to adopt a bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

It should be noted that under the current regulations, the competent minister cannot revoke the Deputy Director of the Institute. Only the Director of the Institute, the person acting as Director or the person acting as Head of the Institute may do so. The proposed solution therefore guarantees the highest quality of management. The project also adds new regulations concerning the examination of legality and the statute of the Institute of resolutions of the Scientific Council. These regulations are intended to ensure that the Minister oversees the actual implementation of the supervision of the Institute's activities. The project has a positive socio-economic impact, has no financial impact and does not provide for public expenditure. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-16-sejm-30-2.u343	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the draft law amending the Law on Research Institutes, print No. 995. The project concerns an increase in the competence of the Minister for Supervision, inter alia. as regards the appointment and cancellation of the Director of the Institute and its Deputy, matters relating to the day-to-day management of the Research Institute in the event of the appointment or cancellation of the Director, the possibility of repealing resolutions of the Scientific Council which are not in conformity with the laws and statutes of the Institute. The Sejm of the Republic has recently passed a law on the amendment of certain laws defining the conditions for conducting innovative activities, the purpose of which is to launch instruments that will contribute to the innovation of the Polish economy and thus to the increase of national income and the improvement of the level of prosperity in Poland. The bill on amending the Law on Research Institutes in our assessment is a supplement to the above mentioned law. legislation through the introduction of legal instruments and organisational frameworks to improve innovation for the benefit of industry and the economy globally. In the Responsible Development Strategy, the causes of low innovation were diagnosed. Most of them are systemic and result from insufficient coordination of R &amp; D activities and support instruments. The problem is, above all, insufficient cooperation between institutions carrying out public tasks in the field of innovation with businesses and business environment organisations. It is therefore appropriate to say that research institutes do little to their mission of bringing science closer to business. The consequence of this is a low tendency to cooperate with Polish companies with the world of science, which to a large extent affects their innovation. Studies show that cooperation is often seen by entrepreneurs as a formal requirement for obtaining grants. For these reasons, the Responsible Development Strategy points to the need to reform research institutes in terms of profiling institutes according to sectoral specialisations and ensuring the possibility of conducting interdisciplinary research. The transformation and consolidation of institutes will aim to create players capable of competing on the global market. This process must be accompanied by appropriate instruments to enable effective supervision of research institutes where the Director of the Institute does not perform its functions properly. This situation threatens in the case of institutes performing important tasks for the planning and implementation of state policy, when there is a threat of disruption and delays in the achievement of the development goals of the Polish economy. In this context, the proposed solutions should lead to the strengthening of the minister's supervision, resulting in greater economic innovation and efficiency in the use of public funding for research. We believe that leaving the R &amp; D sector in an organisational drift would be a serious omission of the current government team, which is why we are in favour of providing the Minister supervising the sector with instruments for the appointment and cancellation of the Director of the Research Institute without the participation of the Scientific Council. In our view, this solution will give the Minister greater opportunities to make the right choice for the highest positions at the Institute and, consequently, lead to an improvement in the quality of the Institute's management staff. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-16-sejm-30-2.u362	Thank you. Please respond to the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Finance of Mr Wiesław Jasiński.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-16-sejm-30-2.u363	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Gentlemen! Thank you for asking. With regard to the scale of the reduction in excise tax revenue resulting from the deterioration of the value of passenger cars purchased outside the country, the results of analyses which are relatively rarely carried out can be used. The problem you are addressing is extremely rarely addressed in various types of diagnosis, research. The gray zone raises the interest of important audit entities, and here we are dealing with a deficit. However, such analyses are carried out at the Ministry of Finance. We have analysed this on the example of the most popular models of cars, which are most often imported by Poles. This analysis compared the market value of these vehicles with the value declared for payment of excise duty. In the case of vehicles with an engine capacity above 2 l, i.e. 2000 cm3, i.e. those which are taxed at a lower rate of excise duty, there were no significant differences between the market value and that indicated in the tax return. On the other hand, lowering the tax base is a particularly common practice for cars with an engine capacity above 2 l, as the excise duty rate in this case is 18.6% the values of the vehicle. Let me remind you that this rate was raised from 13.6% on 1 January 2009. This was done by the government of the Civic Platform and PSL. To illustrate this problem, you can use an example of an A6 Audi A6 with an engine capacity of 3000 cm3 produced in 2004 - 12 years old. The average age of cars imported by Poles is 11 years and 9 months. The average value of such a vehicle indicated in excise declarations - and we checked a large representative sample of cars just A6 volume of 3000 cm3, once again - was PLN 13 932 and the tax calculated and paid on such amount is PLN 2591. On the other hand, the market value of such a vehicle is PLN 30 707. The excise duty, which should be paid, amounts to PLN 5711 and thus twice as much. With some brands out of these nine above 2 l this underdog was even greater. When comparing the value of excise duty - the one declared and actually paid with the one potentially to be paid - it can be seen that the excise duty calculated on the basis of the undervalued value of the vehicle on average amounts to around 50% of the amount of tax that should be paid if the market value of the vehicle were taken into account. If this observed correctness were passed on to all cars with an engine capacity above 2 l acquired internally - and I must point out that this would be a significant simplification - it can be estimated that the reduction in budget revenue for under-values of passenger cars imported to Poland can reach up to PLN 400 million per year. This amount should be combined with revenues from passenger car taxation, which amounted to PLN 1894 million in 2015. At the same time, it should be pointed out that cars with an engine capacity above 2 l represent approximately 10% the number of all vehicles that are purchased internally and are responsible for around 45% revenue from this. On the other hand, with regard to the question raised in the question of mechanisms and practices relating to circumvention of the provisions of the Excise Tax Act when purchasing intra-Community cars by lowering the tax base, it is necessary to repeat once again that, due to the current tax system, passenger cars with a capacity exceeding 2 l - 18,6 established by the Platform and PSL on 1 January 2009. One of the patterns of illegal conduct consists in disassembling selected components of the vehicle or its equipment, e.g. leather seats, aluminium rims, disassembly of certain parts and assembly destroyed so as to reduce the value of the vehicle. Such a car is then presented for the valuation of the valuer. After the valuation, the previously mentioned parts shall be re-assembled. Another mechanism for lowering the tax base is to indicate in the excise declaration a much lower value than the market value. The latter mechanism does not require valuations carried out by the assessor. In these cases, the correct tax base shall be established by the tax authorities in the course of the verification of declarations and of the tax proceedings taken. These activities involve significant administrative resources as each of the cases in question is individual and the decision of the tax authority in the case is made following an examination of the rules and the facts. In addition to the previously described mechanisms of circumvention of the provisions relating to vehicle impairment, the practice of registration of passenger cars as heavy goods vehicles has also been defined. This mechanism is linked to the fact that the existing provisions of the Excise Tax Act use the autonomous definition of a passenger car which refers to the classification of the Combined Nomenclature (CN). In summary, there are various mechanisms, [[♪ The bell ♪]] documentation and technical, including the use of legal gaps, those concerning registration of trucks as passenger cars, the largest number of old cars imported in Europe - it is in Poland, a major risk on the roads for this reason and...

First of all, I would like to say that right now there is another 22nd session of the Conference of the Parties to the Climate Agreement. This session takes place in Marrakesh, Morocco. The Conference of the Parties to the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change in Paris has adopted the text of the new international agreement, so-called. the Paris Agreement. I would like to say that the Paris Agreement is a breakthrough in the current climate negotiations, because it not only sets a certain emission limit, but also a way of developing carbon dioxide, among other things. through the soil and through the woods. I would like to stress that the Paris Agreement has been ratified, among others. by the United States and China, and these countries have not yet ratified the Kyoto Convention. During the negotiations Polish representatives were guided by the overriding interests of the state, including economic interests. The new agreement does not jeopardise Poland's economic interests or affect the consistency of its obligations with the priorities of the Polish government. The agreement introduces a new concept of climate neutrality, actively promoted by the Polish delegation, expressed as a desire to balance the emissions of anthropogenic greenhouse gases with their absorption, including by forests. The new concept of climate neutrality has so far replaced the so-called concept of climate neutrality. total decarbonisation of economies. In other words, it will be possible to burn, of course, reducing emissions, fossil fuels, while at the same time it is necessary to absorb these emissions by forests. This approach to achieving the long-term objective eliminates the risks arising from actions aimed at eliminating the total use of fossil fuels in the coming decades, which in the case of Poland would be practically impossible due to the current energy mix. This means that Poland has full freedom to decide what measures it will take to implement the agreement. She's not coerced, for example. to abandon coal mining or the construction of modern coal blocks. This was possible thanks to the active efforts of the Environment Minister in the European Group during the COP, and his high position and international trust will allow, among others. to entrust it by the COP Presidency to chair the work of one of the working groups in the negotiations. During COP21 in Paris, Poland was promoted as a leader in the implementation of the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change. Among other things, it was stressed. five times the CO2 reduction as shown by the Kyoto Protocol - 30% instead of 6%, while increasing GDP by 100% , as well as two COP organisations - in Poznań in 2008 and Warsaw in 2013. The Paris Agreement is a breakthrough in the global approach to climate change. For the first time, it has been possible to negotiate a universal agreement whereby all countries of the world will join measures to reduce global temperature increases. The universality of the agreement makes it easier for Poland to maintain the competitiveness of its economy on the world stage. Furthermore, the ratification of the Agreement by the various parties exceeded the expectations, allowing it to enter into force on 4 November 2016, i.e. before the next session of the Conference of the Parties, just over six months after its signature on 22 April 2016 and less than a year after its adoption. The Agreement was ratified by 109 countries and the European Union by 15 November. As far as the specific solution for emissions trading is concerned, it is at this moment that a session is being held in Morocco, which has been recognised as an implementation session. In other words, it has been signed and implemented, ratified by 109 countries in the world and by the European Union, in order to establish certain details at the subsequent conferences of the parties, it can be said, technically, how to implement this convention. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The criterion is so formulated at the moment that in order to access the network, you need to have two profiles from this branch. As regards funds at the disposal of the Minister for the Environment - in quotes at his disposal, because they are at the disposal of different entities, but as regards those that are supervised by the Minister for the Environment - this is primarily the Operational Programme of the Infrastructure and Environment, actions 2.1, 2.4, 2.5, as well as the Norwegian Funds and LIFE+. In addition, there are regional operational programmes. These regional operational programmes are the responsibility of the regional Marshals of the voivodships and, in principle, the Minister of Environment does not have much influence, apart from creating a certain policy. When it comes to how to catch up now, first of all, it's gonna be... The National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management, as the implementing institution, is currently developing changes in EU programmes, mainly in the Operational Programme, Infrastructure and the environment, in such a way as to be able to finance these investments also in the east and south of Poland through financial support from local authorities from national funds, because it has often been so that these investments in poorer municipalities could not be implemented because there was insufficient funding for co-financing. At the moment, the national fund is preparing it. These actions are really very, very much. Now, with regard to water management, about retention tanks, there are no such reservoirs in the partnership agreement, just theoretically they are not yet on the lists, but we are trying to change these criteria - a detailed description of the tasks, as well as a partnership agreement - in such a way as to be able to finance in particular a small retention in non-urbanised areas. This is one of the goals pursued here by both the Minister and the Ministry of the Environment, and the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management, because Poland is a very poor country in water, especially this lack of water feels the east of Poland, there are problems, and on the other hand there are problems with floods. And that's what we're preparing right now. I wanted to say, however, that these changes take time. We're doing a lot right now. I sign a few letters every day in this case because it's a process. Namely, as an intermediary, we must first obtain the approval of the managing authority, the Ministry of Development. We find full understanding there. We're changing all these programs right now. So that I can say that we are also trying to, by means of national measures, as I said earlier... that is, EU funds under the Operational Programme of the State as a whole infrastructure and environment, i.e. designed for the whole country, pursue such a policy, in order to equip these municipalities primarily with sewage treatment plants, because this is one of the most important things, the implementation of the National Urban Waste Water Treatment Programme, and also to conduct comprehensively all other activities related to water management, environmental protection in those areas. Some of these activities are directly addressed to municipal authorities, which themselves decide on the location of new buildings, also taking into account issues related to the provision of appropriate technical infrastructure and access to public transport. This is particularly the case for aid to municipalities in the creation of municipal housing, social housing, shelters or homeless homes. As it seems, the question concerned mainly the activities identified in the National Housing Programme related to the construction of apartments for rent, including the option to acquire housing for property. Housing investments are organised by BGK Real Estate SA. The company mainly cooperates with local authorities, which decide to transfer land property and thus indicate the location of the future investment. So far BGK Real Estate SA, as you know very well, has signed 30 letters of intent with local authorities in the context of the pilotage carried out, which concern most of the specific letters already indicated by the municipality of the location. It is clear from the analysis of the location of these properties that the doubts raised by the Member do not seem justified. New apartments are located nearby or within existing housing estates, with good access to technical and social infrastructure, and in the case of large cities also with access to urban public transport. Currently, investment activity will focus on cooperation with local authorities, at a later stage the housing will also be able to be built on the State Treasury, which are currently on the board of seniors, Agricultural Property Agency and Military Property Agency. It is assumed that new buildings will be built on plots whose use for housing purposes is rational for technical and economic reasons. A condition for the location of housing investments will therefore be the proper classification of municipal areas in planning documents, i.e. local spatial planning plans or studies of conditions and spatial planning for residential construction. Another condition will also be access to the technical infrastructure, appropriate structural features of the land plot - size, shape for the planned investment. As regards the concerns that new investments will be located in areas remote from existing urbanization structures requiring costly infrastructure investments, I would like to ensure that they are unjustified and that local authorities should not bear any additional costs. In addition, the pursuit of the situation that new buildings will be located in areas covered by spatial planning will prevent the spread of cities. Finally, the answer on financial issues: we maintain earlier declarations that the costs of most locations should be in the range of 2.5 000. PLN up to 3000. zł per m2. Of course, in the case of large cities, these costs may be slightly higher, while for the time being additional registered municipalities also carry out appropriate analyses in this respect. We therefore expect, as we wrote in the impact assessment of the regulation, that the increase in the number of children born within 10 years will reach 300 000. and we expect an increase in the fertility rate from the current 1.3 to 1.6, we would, of course, like it to be even more. The results of this programme are still to be waited for for obvious reasons - the programme has been operational since 1 April. But that's why it's so important to emphasize that he is a multi-annual program, because people decide on offspring at a time when - and that's what international research says, the literature of an object - they will actually believe that he is permanently inscribed in the landscape, because then they can make decisions consciously. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-16-sejm-30-2.u424	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! The question relates to the Act of 27 June 2003 on the social pension. OJ 2003 No 135, p. 1268, as regards the suspension of the right to a social pension. In view of the previous government initiatives of Prime Minister Beata Szydło in favour of the environment and people with disabilities themselves, I would like to ask the question whether the government is considering the possibility of amending the legal provisions on the elimination of the limits of the social benefit payment introduced by Article. 10 social disability laws. It is this article that provides that the right to a social pension shall be suspended in the month in which the disabled person has earned revenue in the total amount of more than 70% an average wage. At present, this limit is PLN 2927.10 gross.
