ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-16-sejm-30-2.u425	I ask the Minister to respond.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-16-sejm-30-2.u426	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The rules governing the granting and payment of social pensions, as the Member said, are governed by the Act of 27 June 2003 on the social pension. According to Art. 4 of this law is entitled to an adult who is completely incapable of working because of an impairment of the body's fitness, which was created before the age of 18 during schooling or at a higher school before the age of 25, or - the third possibility - during doctoral studies or scientific aspirate. The benefit in the form of a social pension is intended for people who, in most cases, never attain independence, live with their parents at adult age or stay in institutional forms of care for many years, such as. Social services homes. These conditions for the acquisition of the right to a social pension mean that the pension is intended for persons who, because of their previous incapacity for work, have not been able to take up a job and have therefore not acquired their own pension rights for incapacity for work and often even the possibility to acquire any professional qualifications. In 2012, in a revised social pension law, in a revised article. Article 10 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 6, the right to a social pension shall be suspended for the month in which the total income of more than 70% has been earned the average monthly salary for the calendar quarter recently announced by the President of the CSO for pension purposes. Currently, from 1 September 2016 to 30 November, the suspension shall be made after the revenue of PLN 2813 has been exceeded. Let us remind you that the minimum wage will be 2 000. zł. It is estimated - these are estimates - that already in the current legal state the number of insured persons entitled to a social pension at the same time as at the end of September this year is around 31 thousand, i.e. represents around 12% the total number of persons receiving the pension. It should be noted, however, that the social pension is a compulsory, continuous and protective provision, and its aim is to compensate for the impossibility of acquiring entitlement to benefits from the social security system because this total incapacity for work arose before entering the labour market. Social pensions as benefits from the social security system are financed entirely from the state budget, as are social benefits. However, it should be noted that the social pension is not an income-dependent benefit, since it is not subject to income criteria, as is the case with social assistance benefits. The amount of social assistance similar to a social pension intended for disabled persons who do not have their own pension benefit, which is a fixed allowance, shall be reduced each time by the amount of income held by the entitled person and his/her family. The rules on the payment of social benefits are much more favourable to insured persons. In addition, the suspension of the right to a social pension lasts only for the period of the income and, in the event of the cessation of income, the right to a social pension shall be restored, i.e. the person whose health condition allows him to obtain higher income from work, may take up work without fear that he will not be able to return to that benefit in the future. In view of the above, the Council of Ministers does not plan to take a legislative initiative to remove the limits for making up for social pensions. At the same time, I would like to point out that since 2017 there will be a really unprecedented increase in the amount of social pensions. This is linked to an increase in the amount of the minimum pension. Thus, from 1 March 2017, this pension will amount to PLN 840. Today, on 1 March 2016, it is 741,35 PLN. If we compare the amounts since 2006, we will notice that the increase was by PLN 10.20, here we have a year to year over PLN 100. This is an unprecedented increase in the amount of this pension. Thank you very much.

In these 90 there are specific investments that are on the list that are estimated and for which there is simply no money. Today we have to make decisions that we will be making today, which - tomorrow - the day after tomorrow and which - will be the day after tomorrow. I speak the common language, of course, not the planning language, because it works for years, not for individual days. These are, ladies and gentlemen, the main dilemmas. Maybe road S3. Here many questions were asked about the road S3 on the section of Bolków - Lubabek. Ladies and gentlemen, the decision has been made. This has been addressed to the realization. So that's it, period. The decision has been made. We're working to get the tenders out as soon as possible, so it'll just go off. This is not a second stage referral, a tender that has already been placed and some work has already been done before, but these are the concrete decisions that our government is making. Indeed, there is, I do not hide, a difficult problem, because individual environments, individual cities, individual towns, subregions would like to be connected to express roads, to two-band communication arteries. There is a problem not only financially, but also environmentally. Certain solutions simply cannot be achieved without being exposed to environmental problems. As they can be burdensome, you can see, for example, after the construction of S7, Skarżysko-Kamienna, where the construction of one node really stopped. Environmentally, the case is still unsolved. Apart from environmental problems, there are still various social issues - as regards Podlasie - as well as the fact that we want Via Carpatia to have an optimal course and to be accepted as a communication artery not only for Podlasie, but also for the whole of Poland and in the perspective of what we are talking about in the topic of Via Carpatia, to be accepted by all travelling across the eastern border, at the eastern border of the European Union. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-16-sejm-30-2.u574	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On 25 November this year. for 70. the anniversary of the last great underground action, i.e. The reflections of the unbreakable soldiers from the Pultusk prison. I would like to take this opportunity to say a few words about the circumstances of this event. In the autumn of 1946, the arrests of the Public Security Office affected the Resistance of the National Army from the area of the Pułtuski district and the vicinity of Wyszkow. As a result, the decision was made to release the prisoners by force - by breaking up the prison in Pułtusk, to which they were transported. About 40 men were involved in the action, which consisted of patrols: pr. Stanisława. Translucent of Lanecki - from the district of Ostrów Mazowiecka - Stanisława.Visa. Suminskiego, the distribution group of WiN Łochów Jana The operation was performed incredibly smoothly. In the afternoon of November 25, 1946, WiN soldiers dressed in uniforms of the People's Polish Army entered Pułtusk from the western side and stopped on the market. After dark they left the cars and stations equipped with machine guns and blocked the headquarters of the Public Security Office and the Civic Militia and two bridges on the channel. At the same time, a second group of WiN soldiers entered the city. About an hour. One of the patrols took over the mail and crashed the switchboard. That's why communications have been cut off. Later, some soldiers entered the building where the prison workers lived and disarmed Jan Dwornik - the prison manager. The manor brought the guerrillas into prison and helped free the prisoners from their cells. During the action, the crew was disarmed and all political prisoners were released. Some sources say that 48 people were released, others that there were up to 70. In prison three death sentences were executed on guards who were extremely cruel to prisoners. In total, seven bellies and two ormites were killed, and several officers were wounded. At the same time, the guerrillas captured large amounts of weapons, among others. PPSz grenades and machine guns, commonly referred to as pepesh. While the prisoners left their cells, guerrilla insurance blocked all attempts to get into prison with the fire of machine guns. When there was a disturbance at the bridges on the canal and the security forces went through them, Lieutenant. Stanisław Lanecki ordered the retreat. The branch confused the operational groups of KBW and went towards the Ostrów district. The group managed to leave town. The resort was so surprised by these actions that it did not organize an effective chase. Unfortunately, during his escape, he was sent to two security officers. And even though he managed to shoot both of them, he was wounded to death. The capture of the prisoners was of great propagandism. She made a great impression on the population. Despite the fatal wound, it should be regarded as a successful wound. The WiN Guerrilla Branch of the Ostrów Mazowiecka Region ceased its operations in April 1947, revealing itself during the so-called. Amnesty. However, several of his soldiers decided to continue the fight and moved on to another unit. Hello and praise the heroes! Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-16-sejm-30-2.u576	Statement on amendments to the Law on cemeteries and the hiding of the dead in connection with the published report of the Supreme Audit Chamber. A recent report by the Supreme Audit Chamber on the management of municipal cemeteries highlights a number of comments that deserve to be amended, particularly in the Law on cemeteries and the burial of the dead of 31 January 1959. On the website of the Supreme Audit Chamber, we can read that controlled local authorities have often ignored the management of municipal cemeteries. Most of them were confined to basic matters, i.e. to providing burial sites and cleanliness control. The remaining tasks were commissioned outside without checking how - and even whether - they were carried out. As a result, against the law, cemeteries managers gained more powers than they should have. They set themselves up, for example. prices for burial sites or at the same time with cemetery municipal services, despite the statutory ban, carried out commercial funeral services. My greatest attention in the published report was drawn to the fact that there is no definition of the concept of cemetery services and, consequently, the catalogue of fees associated with the implementation of these services. The report says that there is often a violation of the law, and that people close to the dead are surprised by the high cemetery fees and the disunity of local authorities' actions. As an example, the total cost of the burial place for a double grave for a period of 20 years in the Wielkopolska Voivodeship in one city amounted to 255 PLN and in the second city - 1980 PLN. In connection with the published report of the Supreme Audit Chamber, I ask the High Chamber to work on amending the Act by centralising the rates in such a way that there is not such a glaring imbalance, and to introduce a special catalogue of cemetery charges. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-16-sejm-30-2.u577	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On 14 October we celebrated the National Education Day, a special celebration in the Polish calendar, as it is the date commemorating the creation of the National Education Committee in 1773. It was an institution of great importance in shaping the education system in Poland, as well as an example for the whole of Europe, so that education has a significant role in the functioning of the state. The Commission was the first Ministry responsible for public education in our country, as well as the first institution of this type in Europe designed to rebuild the education system in the Republic of Poland. It was founded mainly because by 1773, primary and secondary education were organized and controlled in Poland by the Jesuit order, which characterized the contemporary conservativeism and religious intolerance. After many centuries since the creation of the prototype of the Ministry of National Education, we appreciate the huge contribution of this institution in shaping patriotic and social attitudes, i.e. those qualities which proved to be important in the survival of the Polish state in the years to come. Education is not only science, it is primarily the upbringing of a young man and an indication of noble and timeless values that will guide the young generation throughout their lives and which they will guide in making often difficult decisions. At this point, attention should be paid to those who can make sure that our children receive not only adequate knowledge but also important values and patriotic education for many years. It is thanks to you, dear teachers and educators that many young people can discover and develop their predispositions, passions and talents, find motivation to fight weaknesses, and find themselves in today's difficult reality. It is also your dedication and commitment that encourages young people to gain knowledge, ask questions and seek answers to them. I wish you that your daily work is a source of satisfaction and a sense of a well-fulfilled mission, and the attachment and gratitude of your students will reward your hard work. Thank you all for your commitment and enthusiasm in the process of creating the best quality work of educational units, which are becoming more interesting, more friendly and open. May you be accompanied every day by the serenity and passion to pass on knowledge to your students and influence their life choices. In the shadow of recent events and planned reform, I also wish that all things will be successful, and on my part I declare my full support not to destroy what you have been building over the years. I therefore ask Minister Anna Zalewska to withdraw from the changes contained in the draft laws so that she can finally draw attention to the voice of teachers on this issue. In addition, I would like to ask the Minister to lean on the problem of lack of consultation, lack of work and lack of information on how the reform will affect the employment of teachers in Poland. The proposals with which the Ministry of National Education comes are unwise. The idea of eliminating middle school and returning to the old formula are actions that will devastate our education. Unprepared schools in which there are no physical, chemical studios, gymnasiums or school fields, especially small schools located in rural areas, are still unsolved, resulting in only the education of our children. At the same time, I call on Prime Minister Beata Szydło to stop work on education reform. In the case of such a profound and important reform, I ask that all the environments affected by this law be discussed. I would also ask that these changes be preceded by a very profound analysis of the effects that will arise after its adoption. The whole harm to these changes will directly affect the education of our children and grandchildren, which is why it is extremely important that such reforms be carried out in a very responsible manner, which we cannot say about this project.
